- Active motifs: concealment from sight (quranic:root_001117:B001/m01); chest as inner enclosure (quranic:root_000849:B001/m01); chest as source of outward acts (quranic:root_000849:B004/m01); hidden heart or inward core (quranic:root_000266:B010/m01); knowledge of inner conditions (quranic:root_000387:B001/m01); comprehensive knowledge (quranic:root_001040:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:31 `خَبِيرٌۢ` (خ ب ر); 35:33 `جَنَّٰتُ` (ج ن ن); 35:38 `غَيْبِ/عَٰلِمُ/عَلِيمٌۢ/صُّدُورِ` (غ ي ب/ع ل م/ص د ر); 35:44 `عَلِيمًا` (ع ل م).
- Synthesis: Hiddenness is a limit on ordinary reception, not on knowability. Chest, heart, and inner condition locate the source from which action issues, making later judgment responsive to concealed formation as well as visible deed.

#### Subchannel B. Sight, insight, and evidentiary showing
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Eyes receive an object, reflection converts reception into insight, and a claimant is required to bring the alleged object into manifest view.
- Active motifs: visual perception (quranic:root_000121:B001/m01); inward discernment or insight (quranic:root_000121:B002/m01); causing something to be seen (quranic:root_000531:B012/m01); reflective looking (quranic:root_001520:B001/m01); emergence into view (quranic:root_000970:B001/m01); manifestation as proof (quranic:root_000170:B004/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:31 `بَصِيرٌ` (ب ص ر); 35:40 `رَءَيْ/أَرُ/بَيِّنَتٍ` (ر ء ي/ب ي ن); 35:43-44 `يَنظُرُ` (ن ظ ر); 35:45 `بَصِيرًۢا/ظَهْرِ` (ب ص ر/ظ ه ر).
- Synthesis: Seeing is partitioned into reception, reflective interpretation, and demonstrative showing. The demand to show what partners created therefore exposes a failure of evidence, not merely a failure to look.

### 20. P3 (35:31-45) Fire, Death, and Failed Exit
- Semantic invariant: Punishment becomes a closed process when fire persists, death is withheld as an endpoint, relief is denied, and cries fail to establish an exit relation.
- Surface relation: direct; 35:36-37 describes fire, uncompleted death, unlightened punishment, tasting, cries, and a rejected request to leave.
- Surprising reach: Legal completion, sensory tasting, aid, and spatial exit separate several ways an ordeal might terminate and show each one failing independently.

#### Subchannel A. Fire without terminal death
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Fire is experienced as punishment, but the decree that would complete life is withheld and the burden of the ordeal is not reduced.
- Active motifs: consuming fire (quranic:root_001564:B002/m01); decree brought to completion (quranic:root_001237:B003/m01); completion or expiration of a term (quranic:root_001237:B004/m01); loss of life (quranic:root_001454:B001/m01); experiential tasting (quranic:root_000526:B002/m01); painful punishment (quranic:root_000994:B005/m01); reduction of a burden (quranic:root_000427:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:36 `نَارُ/يُقْضَىٰ/يَمُوتُ/يُخَفَّفُ/عَذَابِ` (ن و ر/ق ض ي/م و ت/خ ف ف/ع ذ ب); 35:37 `ذُوقُ` (ذ و ق).
- Synthesis: Death, expiration, and lightening are distinct terminal operations. Their coordinated refusal creates persistence: the fire continues, the sufferer remains alive to experience it, and intensity is not reduced.

#### Subchannel B. Cry, unanswered aid, and blocked departure
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A trapped group raises a loud appeal for aid and asks to cross outward, but neither helper nor exit relation is established.
- Active motifs: loud cry (quranic:root_000855:B001/m01); cry for rescue (quranic:root_000855:B002/m01); answering a cry with aid (quranic:root_000855:B003/m01); departure from an enclosure (quranic:root_000400:B001/m01); external support (quranic:root_001510:B001/m01); deliberate corrective action (quranic:root_001046:B001/m01); prior warning (quranic:root_001488:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:37 `يَصْطَرِخُ/أَخْرِجْ/نَعْمَلْ/نَّذِيرُ/نَّصِيرٍ` (ص ر خ/خ ر ج/ع م ل/ن ذ ر/ن ص ر); 35:42 `نَذِيرٌ` (ن ذ ر).
- Synthesis: The cry lexically contains the expectation of response, yet the scene supplies no answering helper. Requested exit and promised corrective action arrive only after the prior warning, so neither reopens the closed process.

### 21. P3 (35:31-45) Cosmic Retention and Capacity
- Semantic invariant: Ordered space persists because a capable agent restrains its parts from displacement and cannot be defeated by any competing force.
- Surface relation: direct; 35:41 describes the heavens and earth as held against removal, and 35:44 denies that anything can incapacitate God.
- Surprising reach: Holding, instability, escape, hand-power, and cord-like strength materialize cosmic order as an ongoing exertion rather than a static arrangement.

#### Subchannel A. Holding against displacement
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Vast bodies tend toward movement or removal but are actively grasped and restrained in their appointed relation.
- Active motifs: holding and restraint (quranic:root_001424:B001/m01); removal from a place or state (quranic:root_000655:B001/m01); movement and instability (quranic:root_000655:B002/m01); sky as overhead structure (quranic:root_000745:B004/m01); earth beneath the sky (quranic:root_000025:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:38, 35:40-41, 35:44 `سَّمَٰوَٰتِ/أَرْضِ` (س م و/ء ر ض); 35:41 `يُمْسِكُ/تَزُولَ/أَمْسَكَ/زَالَ` (م س ك/ز و ل).
- Synthesis: Stability is recast as successful restraint of possible motion. The heavens and earth are not inert background; their continued relation is the present result of holding against displacement.

#### Subchannel B. Effective capacity and failed escape
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Power is sufficient to execute an intended act, while any object that might resist or flee fails to escape its reach.
- Active motifs: effective capacity to act (quranic:root_001205:B003/m01); strength like joined cord strands (quranic:root_001274:B001/m01); inability or incapacitation (quranic:root_000985:B001/m01); escape beyond another's reach (quranic:root_000985:B002/m01); hand as operative power (quranic:root_001693:B002/m01); bodily or structural strength (quranic:root_000782:B002/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:31 `يَدَيْ` (ي د ي); 35:44 `يُعْجِزَ/قَدِيرًا/قُوَّةً/أَشَدَّ` (ع ج ز/ق د ر/ق و ي/ش د د).
- Synthesis: Capacity is measured by successful execution, not by an abstract attribute. The failed-escape sense of inability places the opposing object inside the same reach that performs cosmic restraint.

### 22. P3 (35:31-45) Historical Tracks and Governed Change
- Semantic invariant: Human succession moves along inherited tracks toward an appointed outcome, while the governing pattern itself resists substitution or diversion.
- Surface relation: direct; 35:39 makes humans successors, 35:43 names an invariant divine pattern, and 35:44-45 directs travel, comparison, and delay to a fixed term.
- Surprising reach: Road wear, doctrinal path, alternation, postponement, and arrival turn history into movement whose local occupants change but whose governing route and endpoint remain stable.

#### Subchannel A. Traveled way and inherited custom
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Repeated movement wears a recognizable road; later travelers follow it as both physical route and inherited practice.
- Active motifs: customary governing path (quranic:root_000750:B001/m01); journeying through land (quranic:root_000769:B001/m01); way or established custom (quranic:root_000769:B002/m01); path or adopted doctrine (quranic:root_000522:B007/m01); straight directed path (quranic:root_001230:B002/m01); road worn by passage (quranic:root_001046:B011/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:32 `مُّقْتَصِدٌ` (ق ص د); 35:33-34 `ذَهَبٍ/أَذْهَبَ` (ذ ه ب); 35:37 `نَعْمَلْ/نَعْمَلُ` (ع م ل); 35:43 `سُنَّتَ/سُنَّتِ` (س ن ن); 35:44 `يَسِيرُ` (س ي ر).
- Synthesis: The invariant pattern is not only a rule but a traveled way made legible by repetition. Physical journey, moral direction, and inherited custom remain distinguishable while explaining why earlier outcomes can instruct later occupants.

#### Subchannel B. Succession without alteration of the pattern
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: One human cohort replaces another and conditions alternate, yet neither substitution nor redirection alters the governing pattern.
- Active motifs: substitution of one thing for another (quranic:root_000095:B001/m01); change of form or condition (quranic:root_000095:B002/m01); transfer from one state to another (quranic:root_000373:B001/m01); succession by replacement (quranic:root_000433:B001/m01); alternation between occupants or states (quranic:root_000433:B006/m01); ordered succession (quranic:root_001033:B005/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:39 `خَلَٰٓئِفَ` (خ ل ف); 35:43 `تَبْدِيلًا/تَحْوِيلًا` (ب د ل/ح و ل); 35:44 `عَٰقِبَةُ` (ع ق ب).
- Synthesis: Human succession is genuine change within history, whereas the governing pattern is the condition under which those changes recur. The explicit denial of substitution and diversion prevents the two levels from being collapsed.

#### Subchannel C. Respite, fixed term, and arrival
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Seizure is postponed for a measured interval, earlier actors do not outrun the later outcome, and the appointed endpoint finally arrives.
- Active motifs: fixed appointed term (quranic:root_000016:B001/m01); postponement of an act (quranic:root_000019:B002/m01); granting time or respite (quranic:root_001520:B002/m01); outcome that follows an act (quranic:root_001033:B006/m01); being earlier in time (quranic:root_001198:B002/m01); coming and arrival (quranic:root_000281:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:37, 35:42, 35:45 `جَآءَ` (ج ي ء); 35:43-44 `يَنظُرُ` (ن ظ ر); 35:44 `قَبْلِ/عَٰقِبَةُ` (ق ب ل/ع ق ب); 35:45 `يُؤَخِّرُ/أَجَلٍ/أَجَلُ` (ء خ ر/ء ج ل).
- Synthesis: Delay changes timing but not destination. The fixed term bounds respite, while arrival and outcome explain why earlier peoples provide evidence even when present seizure has not yet occurred.

### 23. P3 (35:31-45) Moral Accounting
- Semantic invariant: Intended action accrues to its agent and returns as a consequence that can increase rank or compound loss.
- Surface relation: direct; 35:36-39 and 35:43-45 repeatedly join disbelief, action, recompense, increase, loss, plotting, and earned seizure.
- Surprising reach: Commerce, growth, precedence, and earned acquisition make consequence cumulative and agent-bound rather than a single externally imposed event.

#### Subchannel A. Action acquired and returned
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An agent intentionally performs an act, acquires it as part of the self's account, and meets the consequence that follows it.
- Active motifs: intentional action (quranic:root_001046:B001/m01); recompense according to deed (quranic:root_000244:B001/m01); acquisition by one's act (quranic:root_001296:B001/m01); consequence or final outcome (quranic:root_001033:B006/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:36 `نَجْزِى` (ج ز ي); 35:37 `نَعْمَلْ/نَعْمَلُ` (ع م ل); 35:44 `عَٰقِبَةُ` (ع ق ب); 35:45 `كَسَبُ` (ك س ب).
- Synthesis: The sequence preserves agency at every stage: intention produces an act, the act is acquired to its performer, and recompense is the act's returned outcome rather than an unrelated imposition.

#### Subchannel B. Increase, advantage, and compounded loss
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A position can grow into surplus and precedence, while a losing transaction compounds the deficit already attached to its agent.
- Active motifs: general loss or diminution (quranic:root_000409:B001/m01); commercial loss (quranic:root_000409:B002/m01); increase and growth (quranic:root_000657:B001/m01); surplus beyond sufficiency (quranic:root_001163:B001/m01); advantage or higher degree (quranic:root_001163:B002/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:32, 35:35 `فَضْلُ/فَضْلِ` (ف ض ل); 35:39 `خَسَارًا/يَزِيدُ` (خ س ر/ز ي د); 35:42 `زَادَ` (ز ي د).
- Synthesis: Increase and loss are directional accumulations, not binary verdicts. The trade sense of loss makes disbelief a worsening account, while surplus and degree show the opposite possibility of value exceeding its starting measure.

### 24. P3 (35:31-45) Oath, Warning, and Aversion
- Semantic invariant: A maximally strengthened pledge creates an obligation of receptivity, but the arrival of its promised warning can still disclose withdrawal rather than guidance.
- Surface relation: direct; 35:42 reports emphatic oaths to heed a warner and then records greater aversion when a warner arrives.
- Surprising reach: Divine naming, life-stakes adjuration, arrival, and bodily recoil expose the distance between verbal commitment and enacted response.

#### Subchannel A. Strengthened pledge
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Speakers invoke the divine name and bind themselves with their strongest oath, treating the pledge as an obligation that must later be discharged.
- Active motifs: oath as binding speech (quranic:root_001226:B004/m01); oath used for confirmation (quranic:root_001698:B003/m01); divine name invoked in oath (quranic:root_000047:B002/m01); adjuration by life (quranic:root_001044:B002/m01); discharging a sworn obligation (quranic:root_000351:B005/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:31 `ٱللَّهَ` (ء ل ه); 35:35 `أَحَلَّ` (ح ل ل); 35:37 `نُعَمِّرْ` (ع م ر); 35:42 `أَقْسَمُ/أَيْمَٰنِ` (ق س م/ي م ن).
- Synthesis: The oath is a speech act with maximal force, not evidence of later compliance. Invoked name, life-stakes force, and discharge sharpen the subsequent failure by specifying what the speakers claimed to bind.

#### Subchannel B. Warning arrives, response recoils
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A warning reaches its addressees with directional guidance, but reception turns into bodily and affective withdrawal.
- Active motifs: warning before consequence (quranic:root_001488:B001/m01); coming or arrival (quranic:root_000281:B001/m01); aversion and recoil (quranic:root_001532:B001/m01); guidance toward a way (quranic:root_001583:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:37, 35:42, 35:45 `جَآءَ` (ج ي ء); 35:37, 35:42 `نَّذِيرُ/نَذِيرٌ` (ن ذ ر); 35:42 `نُفُورًا/أَهْدَىٰ` (ن ف ر/ه د ي).
- Synthesis: Arrival completes the condition anticipated by the oath, but not its promised response. Guidance offers a direction of movement; aversion names movement in the opposite direction.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. P1 (35:1-14) Kindled Heat and Consuming Fire
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Heat intensifies until material catches, fire consumes what is fed to it, and ignition can expand into open conflict.
- Active motifs: intense heat (quranic:root_000014:B002/m01); fire consuming its fuel (quranic:root_000043:B005/m01); kindling a fire (quranic:root_000708:B001/m01); kindling war (quranic:root_000708:B002/m01); transformation by burning (quranic:root_000862:B009/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:6 `سَّعِيرِ` (س ع ر); 35:10 `يَصْعَدُ` (ص ع د); 35:12 `أُجَاجٌ/تَأْكُلُ` (ء ج ج/ء ك ل).
- Synthesis: The latent scene pressures the adversarial summons of 35:6 by giving fire a process: heat, fuel, ignition, and transformation. The war sense remains a distinct escalation of kindling rather than a synonym for combustion.

### S2. P1 (35:1-14) Cordage, Binding, and Joined Ends
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Fibers are doubled into cord, tightened as restraint or fastening, and joined end-to-end to hold separated parts in relation.
- Active motifs: doubled rope or rein (quranic:root_000208:B007/m01); shackle or restraining cord (quranic:root_000582:B009/m01); sandal thong (quranic:root_000791:B004/m01); long fastening rope (quranic:root_000796:B002/m01); dangling end or appendage (quranic:root_000994:B006/m01); joining two facing parts (quranic:root_001198:B010/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:1 `مَّثْنَىٰ` (ث ن ي); 35:4 `قَبْلِ` (ق ب ل); 35:6 `شَّيْطَٰنَ` (ش ط ن); 35:7 `عَذَابٌ` (ع ذ ب); 35:10 `يَرْفَعُ` (ر ف ع); 35:14 `شِرْكِ` (ش ر ك).
- Synthesis: Doubling, fastening, and joining form a material channel of relation under tension. It gives the otherwise slight image of the date-stone membrane in 35:13-14 a neighboring vocabulary of fibers and appendages without merging those distinct objects.

### S3. P1 (35:1-14) Distance, Rupture, and Nearness
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An object is brought near or held below a boundary, while another is driven away until relation becomes rupture or ruin.
- Active motifs: spatial distance (quranic:root_000131:B001/m01); causing separation (quranic:root_000131:B003/m01); ruin figured as being driven far away (quranic:root_000131:B004/m01); remoteness and cutoff (quranic:root_000796:B001/m01); nearness (quranic:root_000493:B001/m01); proximity below a limit (quranic:root_000502:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:5 `دُّنْيَا` (د ن و); 35:6 `شَّيْطَٰنَ` (ش ط ن); 35:9 `بَعْدَ` (ب ع د); 35:13 `دُونِ` (د و ن).
- Synthesis: Distance is not merely location: it can be actively produced until companionship and access fail. The near/below-limit motif in 35:13 keeps the invoked powers spatially proximate in formulation while 35:14 makes them relationally unreachable.

### S4. P1 (35:1-14) Accountability, Judgment, and Cover
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A harmful act is exposed to reckoning and judgment, while forgiveness covers the act or shields its consequence.
- Active motifs: accountability for fault (quranic:root_000018:B002/m01); painful punishment (quranic:root_000994:B005/m01); forgiveness as protection (quranic:root_001096:B002/m01); decisive judgment (quranic:root_000348:B002/m01); opening a dispute by judgment (quranic:root_001124:B003/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:2 `حَكِيمُ/يَفْتَحِ` (ح ك م/ف ت ح); 35:6 `ٱتَّخِذُ` (ء خ ذ); 35:7 `عَذَابٌ/مَّغْفِرَةٌ` (ع ذ ب/غ ف ر).
- Synthesis: The channel distinguishes exposure to judgment from the protective operation that could cover consequence. The opening sense of judgment usefully reverses the mercy-opening of 35:2: opening may release a gift or initiate a decisive case.

### S5. P1 (35:1-14) Celestial Measures of Time
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Moon, night, and daylight divide duration into observable stations and recurring periods.
- Active motifs: moon and reflected light (quranic:root_001255:B001/m01); lunar station or measured stage (quranic:root_000148:B004/m01); full-moon night (quranic:root_000766:B012/m01); night as darkness and time (quranic:root_001392:B001/m01); opening into daylight (quranic:root_001559:B002/m01); daytime as manifest interval (quranic:root_001700:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:9 `بَلَدٍ` (ب ل د); 35:12 `يَسْتَوِى` (س و ي); 35:13 `لَّيْلَ/نَّهَارِ/قَمَرَ` (ل ي ل/ن ه ر/ق م ر); 35:14 `يَوْمَ` (ي و م).
- Synthesis: The subjection of sun and moon in 35:13 is reframed as temporal governance: light-bearing bodies make duration legible through stations and alternation. The full-moon branch adds a peak phase without collapsing moon, night, and day into one motif.

### S6. P2 (35:15-30) Replacement by a New Creation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An existing population departs, another newly made population arrives, and succession preserves continuity while changing occupants.
- Active motifs: bringing into creation (quranic:root_000434:B002/m01); newness after nonexistence (quranic:root_000227:B004/m01); successor replacing a predecessor (quranic:root_000433:B001/m01); alternation between occupants (quranic:root_000433:B006/m01); coming or arrival (quranic:root_000009:B001/m01); departure from a place (quranic:root_000522:B006/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:16 `يُذْهِبْ/يَأْتِ/بِخَلْقٍ/جَدِيدٍ` (ذ ه ب/ء ت ي/خ ل ق/ج د د); 35:27-28 `مُّخْتَلِفًا/مُخْتَلِفٌ` (خ ل ف).
- Synthesis: Replacement is decomposed into departure, arrival, new production, and succession. This keeps 35:16 from reading as simple destruction: the operative image is a controlled exchange of occupants.

### S7. P3 (35:31-45) Deceptive Promise and Recoiling Harm
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A glittering promise creates false hope, matures into a concealed harmful plan, and finally turns back upon its maker as loss.
- Active motifs: deception by attractive appearance (quranic:root_001078:B008/m01); promise that creates expectation (quranic:root_001662:B001/m01); threatened outcome (quranic:root_001662:B002/m01); evil descending or encircling its agent (quranic:root_000381:B001/m01); adverse turn or defeat (quranic:root_000499:B004/m01); loss and diminution (quranic:root_000409:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:35 `دَارَ` (د و ر); 35:39 `خَسَارًا` (خ س ر); 35:40 `غُرُورًا/يَعِدُ` (غ ر ر/و ع د); 35:43 `يَحِيقُ` (ح ي ق).
- Synthesis: Promise and threat are separated as opposed expectations generated by the same speech relation. Glitter conceals the harmful trajectory, while encirclement explains how the plotted evil returns to the agent who projected it outward.

### S8. P3 (35:31-45) Marital Household and Bodily Life Cycle
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A household relation is formed through marriage, shared residence, clothing, and affinity, then persists through aging and possible abandonment.
- Active motifs: unmarried or abandoned woman (quranic:root_000180:B007/m01); wedding night before consummation (quranic:root_000306:B016/m01); spouse as household companion (quranic:root_000351:B006/m01); affinity formed by marriage (quranic:root_000791:B003/m01); repudiation by marital formula (quranic:root_000970:B010/m01); reaching old age (quranic:root_000985:B003/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:33 `حَرِيرٌ` (ح ر ر); 35:35 `أَحَلَّ` (ح ل ل); 35:40 `شُرَكَآءَ/شِرْكٌ` (ش ر ك); 35:44 `يُعْجِزَ` (ع ج ز); 35:45 `تَرَكَ/ظَهْرِ` (ت ر ك/ظ ه ر).
- Synthesis: These remote senses gather around household formation and its vulnerabilities, offering a human-scale counter-scene to permanent residence in 35:33-35. The channel remains latent: it does not recast the gardens as marriage, but tests permanence against ordinary bonds that age, rupture, or fail.

### S9. P3 (35:31-45) Woven Structure, Fold, and Wear
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Threads or strips interlace into cloth, receive folds and creases, and eventually show wear or altered condition.
- Active motifs: interlacing parts into a structure (quranic:root_000464:B008/m01); first fold of cloth (quranic:root_001226:B007/m01); striped lines like straps (quranic:root_000769:B004/m01); fold or crease (quranic:root_001078:B001/m01); worn cloth (quranic:root_000434:B009/m01); changed or decayed condition (quranic:root_000433:B007/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:33 `يَدْخُلُ` (د خ ل); 35:39 `خَلَٰٓئِفَ` (خ ل ف); 35:40 `غُرُورًا/خَلَقُ` (غ ر ر/خ ل ق); 35:42 `أَقْسَمُ` (ق س م); 35:44 `يَسِيرُ` (س ي ر).
- Synthesis: Interlacing and folding materialize ordered construction at a small scale, while wear supplies a latent model of alteration. This scene usefully pressures the contrast between changeable created structures and the cosmic order held from displacement in 35:41.

### S10. P3 (35:31-45) Stone Surface, Seal, and Inscription
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hard surface is smoothed or sealed, retains water in a hollow, and can receive a durable written mark.
- Active motifs: smooth bright stone (quranic:root_000121:B007/m01); sealed solid rock (quranic:root_000434:B012/m01); clean smooth stone (quranic:root_000873:B006/m01); hard even surface (quranic:root_000852:B002/m01); hard site that retains water (quranic:root_001424:B004/m01); inscription cut into stone (quranic:root_001633:B010/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:31 `بَصِيرٌ/مُصَدِّقًا/أَوْحَيْ` (ب ص ر/ص د ق/و ح ي); 35:32 `ٱصْطَفَيْ` (ص ف و); 35:40 `خَلَقُ` (خ ل ق); 35:41 `يُمْسِكُ` (م س ك).
- Synthesis: The stone senses join hardness with reception: a surface can resist change yet hold water or preserve inscription. That tension offers a material analogue for a stable written revelation and a retained cosmic order without merging those primary scenes.

### S11. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Sowing, Water-Lifting, and Harvest
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A cultivator works an apportioned field with paired-ox yoke bars, covers seed, lifts and routes water, watches grain form, and carries the cut crop to the threshing floor.
- Active motifs: sharecropping and field cultivation (quranic:root_000387:B003/m01); paired-ox plow-yoke bars (quranic:root_000741:B015/m01); covering seed with earth (quranic:root_001307:B008/m01); water-lifting machine (quranic:root_000373:B016/m01); watering field and crop (quranic:root_001438:B005/m01); grain forming in the crop (quranic:root_001348:B011/m01); carrying harvest to the threshing floor (quranic:root_000582:B006/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:7 `كَفَرُ` (ك ف ر); 35:10 `يَمْكُرُ/يَرْفَعُ` (م ك ر/ر ف ع); 35:12 `لَحْمًا` (ل ح م); 35:14 `خَبِيرٍ/يَسْمَعُ` (خ ب ر/س م ع); 35:43 `تَحْوِيلًا` (ح و ل).
- Synthesis: This worked-field sequence materializes the revivified earth of 35:9 and the yield of 35:27 as hidden seed, delivered water, formed grain, and lifted harvest. It also preserves the agency claim of 35:3: cultivators can prepare, irrigate, and gather, but neither the rain nor the life activated by it originates in their tools.

### S12. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Lure, Snare, and Verified Quarry
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hunters go out, draw an animal from its burrow, set a snare, test a bow, pursue successive quarry, verify death after the strike, and identify the catch as food.
- Active motifs: setting out to hunt (quranic:root_000745:B006/m01); drawing an animal from its burrow (quranic:root_000452:B006/m01); hunter's snare (quranic:root_000791:B006/m01); testing a bow by its string and flex (quranic:root_000526:B003/m01); successive taking of game (quranic:root_000993:B008/m01); checking whether struck game has died (quranic:root_001454:B014/m01); prey prepared for eating (quranic:root_000043:B007/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:1 `سَّمَٰوَٰتِ` (س م و); 35:6 `عَدُوٌّ/عَدُوًّا` (ع د و); 35:9 `مَّيِّتٍ/مَوْتِ` (م و ت); 35:12 `تَأْكُلُ` (ء ك ل); 35:14 `شِرْكِ` (ش ر ك); 35:32 `خَيْرَٰتِ` (خ ي ر); 35:37 `ذُوقُ` (ذ و ق).
- Synthesis: The hunt supports the fresh-meat provision of 35:12 by supplying the missing capture and verification process. Its lure-and-snare mechanism also usefully pressures the adorned evil and hostile summons of 35:5-8 and the recoiling plot of 35:43: attraction directs quarry into a capture structure whose danger is concealed until contact.

### S13. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Prepared Perfume and Spreading Scent
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Camphor and aromatic spices are prepared at a perfume brazier, applied as a coating to body or cloth, and released as a fragrance that spreads beyond its source and reaches the sense of smell.
- Active motifs: camphor as perfume (quranic:root_001307:B011/m01); perfuming with aromatic spices (quranic:root_000933:B003/m01); perfume brazier (quranic:root_000973:B012/m01); coating with khaluq perfume (quranic:root_000434:B010/m01); sweet scent spreading outward (quranic:root_001503:B003/m01); receiving and distinguishing scent (quranic:root_000609:B004/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:1 `خَلْقِ` (خ ل ق); 35:7 `كَفَرُ` (ك ف ر); 35:9 `رِّيَٰحَ/نُّشُورُ` (ر و ح/ن ش ر); 35:12 `طَرِيًّا` (ط ر و); 35:28 `عِبَادِ` (ع ب د).
- Synthesis: An unseen fragrance made knowable by its spreading trace materializes the ascent of good speech in 35:10: an inwardly prepared quality leaves its source and becomes perceptible at a distance. The scene also reframes the winds of 35:9 as carriers whose effects disclose what ordinary sight cannot grasp directly.

### S14. Whole Surah (35:1-45) From Fresh Meat to a Seasoned Meal
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Fresh meat passes to a butcher-cook, enters a cooking pot, receives a measured amount of salt, is served in a bowl, tasted, and eaten.
- Active motifs: edible meat (quranic:root_001348:B001/m01); cooking pot and the butcher-cook who tends it (quranic:root_001205:B007/m01); salt added to food or pot (quranic:root_001442:B001/m01); eating bowl or vessel (quranic:root_000043:B013/m01); tasting food (quranic:root_000526:B001/m01); consuming food (quranic:root_000043:B001/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:1 `قَدِيرٌ` (ق د ر); 35:12 `لَحْمًا/مِلْحٌ/تَأْكُلُ` (ل ح م/م ل ح/ء ك ل); 35:37 `ذُوقُ` (ذ و ق).
- Synthesis: The meal directly materializes the fresh meat and saline water of 35:12 as usable provision transformed by proportion, heat, vessel, taste, and ingestion. Provision in 35:3 is thereby pressured toward bodily dependence: possession is not nourishment until material passes through a skilled sequence and becomes consumable.

### S15. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Singer, Answering Voice, Drum, and Cymbal
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A singer raises a melodic line, a second voice follows in response, a one-faced drum establishes pulse, a cymbal rings, and hearers receive the coordinated performance.
- Active motifs: song and melodic voice (quranic:root_001110:B003/m01); raised-voice song (quranic:root_001507:B009/m01); answering voice following a singer (quranic:root_000186:B007/m01); one-faced drum (quranic:root_001281:B012/m01); ringing cymbal (quranic:root_000897:B009/m01); pleasurable hearing of song (quranic:root_000741:B007/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:7 `كَبِيرٌ` (ك ب ر); 35:14 `يَسْمَعُ/سَمِعُ` (س م ع); 35:15 `غَنِىُّ` (غ ن ي); 35:18 `مَصِيرُ` (ص ي ر); 35:29 `يَتْلُ` (ت ل و); 35:35 `نَصَبٌ` (ن ص ب).
- Synthesis: The ensemble usefully pressures the hearing contrasts of 35:14 and 35:22 by separating produced sound, patterned succession, instrumental reinforcement, and receptive hearing. Even loud, ordered, attractive sound does not complete communication unless an addressee can receive it, sharpening the surah's distinction between calling and making hear.

### S16. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Maysir Arrows and Divided Shares
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Players collect marked arrows in a leather vessel, include the named fifth arrow, draw lots over a slaughtered camel, separate the resulting shares, and seek victory through a wager vulnerable to deception.
- Active motifs: vessel holding maysir arrows (quranic:root_000532:B010/m01); fifth maysir arrow (quranic:root_001533:B016/m01); arrow gambling and division of a camel (quranic:root_001694:B007/m01); apportioning a share (quranic:root_001226:B003/m01); gambling victory through deception (quranic:root_001255:B007/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:8 `نَفْسُ` (ن ف س); 35:11 `يَسِيرٌ` (ي س ر); 35:13 `رَبُّ/قَمَرَ` (ر ب ب/ق م ر); 35:42 `أَقْسَمُ` (ق س م).
- Synthesis: This chance-based allocation usefully pressures the world's deception in 35:5 and the increase of loss in 35:39. A visible prize and formally divided shares generate expectation, but the concealed draw and adversarial wager make hoped-for gain structurally capable of becoming another's loss rather than reliable provision.

### S17. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Offering, Destination, and Slaughter Boundary
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A vow binds its maker to an offering, a consecrated animal is directed toward the sanctuary, a rival rite redirects slaughter to an erected stone, and the boundary of proper slaughter distinguishes offering from carcass.
- Active motifs: self-binding vow (quranic:root_001488:B002/m01); offering made to seek nearness to God (quranic:root_001212:B005/m01); consecrated animal sent to the sanctuary (quranic:root_001583:B005/m01); erected stone used for worship and slaughter (quranic:root_001507:B002/m01); edible animal dead without ritual slaughter (quranic:root_001454:B007/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:8 `يَهْدِى` (ه د ي); 35:9 `مَّيِّتٍ/مَوْتِ` (م و ت); 35:18 `تُنذِرُ/قُرْبَىٰٓ` (ن ذ ر/ق ر ب); 35:35 `نَصَبٌ` (ن ص ب).
- Synthesis: The rite materializes the worship and obedience of 35:28-29 as a directed transfer of a valued body, while the rival slaughter-stone usefully pressures the false invocation of 35:13-14. Destination and authorized handling, not material expenditure alone, determine whether the act embodies nearness or diverts devotion toward a powerless recipient.

### S18. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Herd at the Watering Turn
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A herder drives livestock to water, joins a drinking group with an assigned share, draws water at the camels' mouths, returns a still-thirsty animal for another drink, then leads the herd back at evening and encloses it in a pen.
- Active motifs: driving livestock to water (quranic:root_000762:B001/m01); assigned water share (quranic:root_000783:B002/m01); group sharing a watering place (quranic:root_000783:B003/m01); drawing water at camels' mouths (quranic:root_001198:B014/m01); returning camels for a second drink (quranic:root_000464:B007/m01); evening return of livestock (quranic:root_000609:B005/m01); livestock pen (quranic:root_000897:B003/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:4 `قَبْلِ` (ق ب ل); 35:9 `سُقْ/رِّيَٰحَ` (س و ق/ر و ح); 35:12 `شَرَابُ` (ش ر ب); 35:18 `مَصِيرُ` (ص ي ر); 35:33 `يَدْخُلُ` (د خ ل).
- Synthesis: The daily watering cycle reframes provision in 35:3 and human poverty in 35:15 as apportioned dependence: each body requires a share, repeated access, guidance to the source, and protected return. The scene also materializes divine sufficiency by contrast, since the herd's whole routine is organized around needs it cannot satisfy from itself.

### S19. Cross-Pericope P1-P2-P3 Contested Right and Discharge
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: P1 (35:1-14) supplies the written claim and chosen arbitrator; P2 (35:15-30) supplies the continuing right and release from liability; P3 (35:31-45) supplies collection and final discharge. Bridge: a shared legal invariant and causal sequence in which a contested right remains attached until it is documented, adjudicated, satisfied, and cleared.
- Active motifs: opposing claims to the same right (quranic:root_000347:B004/m01); deed recording a right (quranic:root_000516:B008/m01); appointment of an arbitrator (quranic:root_000348:B005/m01); collecting a debt in full (quranic:root_000244:B003/m01); paying and receiving the due right (quranic:root_001237:B006/m01); release from liability (quranic:root_000436:B006/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:2 `حَكِيمُ` (ح ك م); 35:3 `ٱذْكُرُ` (ذ ك ر); 35:5, 35:24, 35:31 `حَقٌّ/حَقِّ/حَقُّ` (ح ق ق); 35:24 `خَلَا` (خ ل و); 35:36 `نَجْزِى/يُقْضَىٰ` (ج ز ي/ق ض ي).
- Synthesis: The legal process materializes the non-transferable burden of 35:18, the full payment of 35:30, and the earned liability of 35:45 as stages of one attached claim. Its cross-pericope force lies in progression rather than topical recurrence: document and arbitration make the obligation legible, while collection and discharge determine whether it remains active.

### S20. Cross-Pericope P1-P2-P3 Promise, Appointment, and Outcome
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: P1 (35:1-14) establishes a promise and its appointed time or place; P2 (35:15-30) supplies attendance or default at the appointment; P3 (35:31-45) tests the promise by enacted confirmation. Bridge: a causal speech-act sequence with a terminal contrast between fulfillment and breach.
- Active motifs: appointment fixing a promise in time or place (quranic:root_001662:B003/m01); reciprocal promise between parties (quranic:root_001662:B004/m01); arriving and assembling at the appointment (quranic:root_001669:B004/m01); making a promise true by action (quranic:root_000852:B004/m01); failure to keep the appointed promise (quranic:root_000433:B005/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:5, 35:40 `وَعْدَ/يَعِدُ` (و ع د); 35:27-28, 35:39 `مُّخْتَلِفًا/مُخْتَلِفٌ/خَلَٰٓئِفَ` (خ ل ف); 35:30 `يُوَفِّيَ` (و ف ي); 35:31 `مُصَدِّقًا` (ص د ق).
- Synthesis: The appointment scene sharpens the contrast between the true divine promise of 35:5 and the deceiver's promise of 35:40. A promise creates expectation, but only arrival and corresponding action verify it; breach reverses the speech act into evidence against its speaker, while fulfillment converts words into an encountered outcome.

### S21. Cross-Pericope P1-P2-P3 Household Line and Estate
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: P1 (35:1-14) supplies the household frame; P2 (35:15-30) supplies mother, following child, and remembered forebears; P3 (35:31-45) supplies surviving descendants and the estate left at death. Bridge: role progression and reversal across generations, as a dependent child becomes a forebear whose possessions and position pass into the surviving line.
- Active motifs: household and kin group (quranic:root_000067:B003/m01); mother as bearer and nurturer (quranic:root_000053:B001/m01); child following its mother (quranic:root_000186:B006/m01); grandparents and ancestry (quranic:root_000227:B009/m01); surviving descendants (quranic:root_001033:B004/m01); estate left by the dead (quranic:root_000180:B006/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:1, 35:43 `أُو۟لِىٓ/أَوَّلِينَ` (ء و ل); 35:16, 35:27 `جَدِيدٍ/جُدَدٌۢ` (ج د د); 35:24, 35:42 `أُمَّةٍ/أُمَمِ` (ء م م); 35:29 `يَتْلُ` (ت ل و); 35:44 `عَٰقِبَةُ` (ع ق ب); 35:45 `تَرَكَ` (ت ر ك).
- Synthesis: The household progression supports 35:11's creation, generation, and measured lifespan, then reframes the Book's inheritance in 35:32 and human succession in 35:39 at the scale of embodied family roles. Continuity belongs to the line rather than to any one holder: nurture produces followers, followers become forebears, and death turns possession into an estate for those who remain.

### S22. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Udder Filling, Milking, and Drying
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Milk descends into an udder and fills it; a small remnant is left to draw down what follows, while the animal may retain the milk or release it in a continuing flow. A milker works with the fingertips until the supply is collected or dries up.
- Active motifs: milk descending into the udder (quranic:root_000351:B009/m01); full udder and abundant milk (quranic:root_000810:B003/m01); remnant left to call down following milk (quranic:root_000478:B003/m01); milk held back in the udder (quranic:root_000582:B007/m01); continuous milk flow (quranic:root_000563:B006/m01); fingertip milking (quranic:root_001165:B005/m01); milk drying up or being cut off (quranic:root_000227:B011/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:35 `أَحَلَّ` (ح ل ل); 35:12, 35:30, 35:34 `تَشْكُرُ/شَكُورٌ` (ش ك ر); 35:6, 35:13-14, 35:18, 35:40 `يَدْعُوا۟/تَدْعُ/دُعَآءَ` (د ع و); 35:10 `يَرْفَعُ` (ر ف ع); 35:1-2, 35:4, 35:9, 35:24-25 `رُسُلًا/مُرْسِلَ/رُسُلٌ/أَرْسَلَ/أَرْسَلْ/رُسُلُ` (ر س ل); 35:1 `فَاطِرِ` (ف ط ر); 35:16, 35:27 `جَدِيدٍ/جُدَدٌۢ` (ج د د).
- Synthesis: The bodily mechanism materializes the opening and withholding of 35:2 and the provision of 35:3 as a resource that can fill, remain retained, descend, and be gathered. The milker can coax and collect the flow but cannot originate lactation, preserving the surah's distinction between handling provision and creating it. The abundance sense of the gratitude root also links thanksgiving in 35:12 and 35:34 to experienced plenitude rather than bare verbal response.

### S23. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Matched Proverb and Unequal Pair
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A speaker casts a saying as a proverb, places one case beside its counterpart, tests whether the two can be made equal, and leaves the comparison as a lesson or sign.
- Active motifs: speech brought out in utterance (quranic:root_001272:B001/m01); proverb cast as a saying (quranic:root_001397:B003/m01); counterpart set beside counterpart (quranic:root_001520:B006/m01); equality tested between two things (quranic:root_000766:B001/m01); proverb retained as lesson and sign (quranic:root_001397:B011/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:34, 35:40 `قَالُ/قُلْ` (ق و ل); 35:14 `مِثْلُ` (م ث ل); 35:43-44 `يَنظُرُ` (ن ظ ر); 35:12, 35:19, 35:22 `يَسْتَوِى` (س و ي).
- Synthesis: The scene supports the paired contrasts of 35:19-22 as a precise teaching operation: blindness is aligned with sight, darkness with light, shade with heat, and the living with the dead so that non-equivalence becomes the lesson. The likeness wording at 35:14 supplies the lexical hinge, while the repeated refusal of equality prevents analogy from flattening the opposed outcomes it is meant to disclose.

### S24. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Benefaction, Thanks, and Repute
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A benefactor gives a good gift beyond strict equivalence; the recipient recognizes the favor, voices praise repeatedly, and helps a good name circulate. Ingratitude reverses the response by concealing the favor.
- Active motifs: benefaction beyond bare justice (quranic:root_000323:B002/m01); generous gift (quranic:root_000452:B005/m01); recognizing favor and thanking its giver (quranic:root_000810:B001/m01); praise opposed to blame (quranic:root_000355:B001/m01); repeated commendation of good acts (quranic:root_000208:B010/m01); good repute spreading among people (quranic:root_000745:B008/m01); ingratitude as covering a favor (quranic:root_001307:B004/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:8 `حَسَنًا` (ح س ن); 35:32 `خَيْرَٰتِ` (خ ي ر); 35:12, 35:30, 35:34 `تَشْكُرُ/شَكُورٌ` (ش ك ر); 35:1, 35:15, 35:34 `حَمْدُ/حَمِيدُ` (ح م د); 35:1 `مَّثْنَىٰ` (ث ن ي); 35:1, 35:3, 35:13, 35:27, 35:38, 35:40-41, 35:44-45 `سَّمَٰوَٰتِ/سَّمَآءِ/مُّسَمًّى` (س م و); 35:7, 35:14, 35:26, 35:36, 35:39 `كَفَرُ/يَكْفُرُ/كَفُورٍ/كَفَرَ/كُفْرُ/كَٰفِرِينَ` (ك ف ر).
- Synthesis: This response circuit supports the surah's movement from praise in 35:1 to the inhabitants' praise in 35:34 and the naming of God as Appreciative in 35:30. Favor becomes socially legible when it is acknowledged and retold; disbelief is the exact reversal, covering what was received and severing gift from giver. The scene therefore makes praise and ingratitude opposed operations on one benefaction rather than unrelated religious labels.

### S25. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Obscured Riddle and Hidden-Hand Reveal
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A challenger deliberately obscures a meaning in a riddle, keeps its object in the hand, and requires the other player to bring the hidden thing out. The response succeeds by production and disclosure rather than unsupported assertion.
- Active motifs: riddling challenge with an obscured answer (quranic:root_000478:B007/m01); deliberate obscuring and confusion of meaning (quranic:root_001049:B004/m01); hand as the concealing and presenting limb (quranic:root_001693:B001/m01); game of bringing out what is held in the hand (quranic:root_000400:B010/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:6, 35:13-14, 35:18, 35:40 `يَدْعُوا۟/تَدْعُ/دُعَآءَ` (د ع و); 35:19 `أَعْمَىٰ` (ع م ي); 35:31 `يَدَيْ` (ي د ي); 35:12, 35:27, 35:37 `تَسْتَخْرِجُ/أَخْرَجْ/أَخْرِجْ` (خ ر ج).
- Synthesis: The game usefully pressures the demand in 35:40 to show what the alleged partners created. A riddle can hide a real answer in a hand and end when the object is produced; the claimants, by contrast, cannot disclose a created object because none is held. Against 35:38, the scene also distinguishes human guessing under concealment from knowledge that already reaches what chests hide.

### S26. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Whorl-Bead Charm and Counter-Rite
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rounded, whorl-like bead is hung as a charm; an incantation claims to restrain a person or turn the person's face toward another, while a counter-rite is performed to release the affliction.
- Active motifs: spindle whorl supplying the rounded charm form (quranic:root_001177:B005/m01); face-turning bead used by a sorceress (quranic:root_001198:B016/m01); restraining incantation or charm (quranic:root_000018:B004/m01); counter-incantation that removes an affliction (quranic:root_001503:B008/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:12 `فُلْكَ` (ف ل ك); 35:4, 35:25, 35:44 `قَبْلِ` (ق ب ل); 35:6, 35:26, 35:45 `ٱتَّخِذُ/أَخَذْ/يُؤَاخِذُ` (ء خ ذ); 35:9 `نُّشُورُ` (ن ش ر).
- Synthesis: The source-attested rite usefully pressures the deceiver's attractive promise in 35:5-8 and the appeal to powerless recipients in 35:13-14. Its claimed influence is routed through a visible token, binding speech, and an answering release rite; the surah's primary claim denies such invoked intermediaries independent possession or response. The counter-rite sense of spreading also reframes resurrection at 35:9 as an unbinding that no talisman performs.

### S27. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Written Manumission by Installments
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person is held as transferable property under an owner's control; a written contract fixes staged payments, and completion changes the person's legal status from enslavement to freedom.
- Active motifs: enslaved person held as property (quranic:root_000973:B001/m01); ownership and power of disposal (quranic:root_001444:B002/m01); manumission contract with installment terms (quranic:root_001283:B005/m01); freedom and release from bondage (quranic:root_000306:B002/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:28, 35:31-32, 35:45 `عِبَادِ` (ع ب د); 35:1, 35:13 `مَلَٰٓئِكَةِ/مُلْكُ/يَمْلِكُ` (م ل ك); 35:11, 35:25, 35:29, 35:31-32, 35:40 `كِتَٰبٍ/كِتَٰبِ/كِتَٰبَ/كِتَٰبًا` (ك ت ب); 35:21, 35:33 `حَرُورُ/حَرِيرٌ` (ح ر ر).
- Synthesis: The contract materializes ownership, writing, payment, and release as a single status-changing practice. It usefully pressures the ownership claim at 35:13: contingent human title can be documented, transferred, and terminated, while the invoked partners possess not even the date-stone membrane. The Book-bearing servants of 35:31-32 are therefore not equated with chattel; the scene instead exposes how limited every created ownership arrangement is beside the surah's account of divine possession and human dependence.

### S28. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Ostrich Nest, Chick, and Abandoned Egg
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An ostrich pair uses a shallow ground scrape, lays an egg, and opens a reproductive fork toward a living chick or an egg left abandoned in open country.
- Active motifs: ostrich ground nest (quranic:root_000148:B012/m01); ostrich as the parent bird (quranic:root_001525:B006/m01); male ostrich (quranic:root_000967:B006/m01); bird egg (quranic:root_000168:B003/m01); ostrich chick (quranic:root_000248:B010/m01); ostrich egg abandoned in the wilderness (quranic:root_000180:B008/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:9 `بَلَدٍ` (ب ل د); 35:3, 35:28 `نِعْمَتَ/أَنْعَٰمِ` (ن ع م); 35:20, 35:32, 35:37, 35:40 `ظُّلُمَٰتُ/ظَالِمٌ/ظَّٰلِمِينَ/ظَّٰلِمُونَ` (ظ ل م); 35:27 `بِيضٌ` (ب ي ض); 35:1, 35:11, 35:39 `جَاعِلِ/جَعَلَ` (ج ع ل); 35:45 `تَرَكَ` (ت ر ك).
- Synthesis: The exposed nesting scene materializes the generation and measured life of 35:11 as a vulnerable transition from parent to egg to chick rather than an automatic continuity. Its abandoned-egg outcome gives concrete pressure to 35:45, where comprehensive seizure would leave no creature: what is left behind in ordinary reproduction remains fully within the reach of the final claim.

### S29. Whole Surah (35:1-45) Wasting Illness, Recovery, and Relapse
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Illness takes hold of a body and wastes its flesh; the patient begins to recover, returns toward a former condition, and appears restored, but a relapse can return the disease or wound to a worse state.
- Active motifs: illness taking hold in the body (quranic:root_000018:B007/m01); wasting disease that strips flesh (quranic:root_000086:B011/m01); convalescence after emaciation (quranic:root_000152:B003/m01); return toward a better bodily condition (quranic:root_000544:B014/m01); recovery and renewed likeness after illness (quranic:root_001397:B012/m01); relapse after improvement (quranic:root_001096:B004/m01)
- Ayah anchors: 35:6, 35:26, 35:45 `ٱتَّخِذُ/أَخَذْ/يُؤَاخِذُ` (ء خ ذ); 35:12 `بَحْرَانِ` (ب ح ر); 35:4 `تُرْجَعُ` (ر ج ع); 35:14 `مِثْلُ` (م ث ل); 35:7, 35:28, 35:30, 35:34, 35:41 `مَّغْفِرَةٌ/غَفُورٌ/غَفُورًا` (غ ف ر). The surface root ب ل ل is unavailable in the bundle, so its convalescence motif cannot receive a token-level anchor.
- Synthesis: This reversible bodily course usefully pressures the surah's two terminal dwellings. Ordinary relief may follow wasting and still relapse; by contrast, fatigue cannot reach the settled inhabitants in 35:35, while the punishment of 35:36 is neither ended by death nor lightened into convalescence. The relapse sense attached to the forgiveness root sharpens permanent relief as more than a temporary improvement.
